```
   260→        assessment = assessments_store.get(dim_key)
   261→        if not isinstance(assessment, dict):
   262→            continue
   263→        ctx = contexts_store.get(dim_key, {})
   264→        positive_headers = [
   265→            str(ins.get("header", "")).strip()
   266→            for ins in ctx.get("insights", [])
   267→            if isinstance(ins, dict) and ins.get("positive")
   268→        ]
   269→        judgment = assessment.get("judgment")
   270→        if not isinstance(judgment, dict):
   271→            judgment = {}
   272→            assessment["judgment"] = judgment
   273→        if positive_headers:
   274→            judgment["strengths"] = positive_headers[:5]
   275→
```

```
1→# Queue Lifecycle
     2→
     3→`desloppify` now treats the queue as one explicit cycle:
     4→
     5→`scan -> review -> workflow -> triage -> execute -> scan`
     6→
     7→## Rules
     8→
     9→- `scan` is a first-class queue phase.
    10→- `review` covers two entry paths:
    11→  - initial subjective assessment after a fresh scan
    12→  - post-execution non-objective review work after objective execution drains
    13→- `workflow` covers post-review workflow items such as score communication,
    14→  score import, and plan creation.
    15→- `triage` follows workflow and exposes only triage stages.
    16→- `execute` exposes only objective fix work and execution clusters.
    17→
    18→## Deferred Disposition
    19→
    20→Deferred disposition is part of the `scan` boundary, not a separate cycle
    21→phase. If deferred temporary skips exist, they block the scan step until the
    22→user reactivates or permanently dispositions them.
    23→
    24→## Persisted Phase vs Safety Net
    25→
    26→The current lifecycle phase is persisted in `plan.refresh_state.lifecycle_phase`
    27→for debuggability and normal transitions. Queue assembly still re-resolves the
    28→phase from current visible items as a safety net, so stale saved phase state
    29→cannot hide the correct next step after out-of-band changes.
    30→
    31→## Completion Authority
    32→
    33→Queue-tracked work is completed by explicit user actions such as
    34→`desloppify plan resolve`, `plan skip --permanent`, or `plan reopen`.
    35→
    36→Scans and review imports may:
    37→- add new work
    38→- reopen previously completed work when it reappears
    39→- corroborate an existing manual resolution
    40→
    41→They do not silently complete open queue-tracked issues just because a detector
    42→stopped reporting them in the latest scan.
    43→
```

```
1→"""TypeScript fixer loader utilities."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "fix_debug_logs",
     9→    "fix_unused_imports",
    10→    "fix_unused_vars",
    11→    "fix_unused_params",
    12→    "fix_dead_useeffect",
    13→    "fix_empty_if_chain",
    14→]
    15→
    16→_EXPORT_MODULES = {
    17→    "fix_debug_logs": ".logs",
    18→    "fix_unused_imports": ".imports",
    19→    "fix_unused_vars": ".vars",
    20→    "fix_unused_params": ".params",
    21→    "fix_dead_useeffect": ".useeffect",
    22→    "fix_empty_if_chain": ".if_chain",
    23→}
    24→
    25→
    26→def __getattr__(name: str):
    27→    module_path = _EXPORT_MODULES.get(name)
    28→    if module_path is None:
    29→        raise AttributeError(f"module {__name__!r} has no attribute {name!r}")
    30→    module = importlib.import_module(module_path, __name__)
    31→    value = getattr(module, name)
    32→    globals()[name] = value
    33→    return value
    34→
    35→
    36→def __dir__() -> list[str]:
    37→    return sorted(set(globals()) | set(__all__))
```

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me write a comprehensive analysis:

## Analysis: "Compatibility Facades on the Hot Path" Queue Item

Based on my investigation, **this is legitimate work, but the problem is much narrower and less problematic than the item suggests.** Here's what I found:

### 1. **Review/importing imports — Mostly NOT using facades on the hot path**

**Import audit:**
- `cmd.py` imports `desloppify.state as state_mod` (which is `/state.py` → `state_compat`) but uses specific functions: `load_state()`, `utc_now()`, `save_state()`, `score_snapshot()`
- `flags.py` imports the same and uses `utc_now()`
- `results.py` imports `state_compat` directly and passes it to `print_review_import_scores_and_integrity()`, but **that function never actually uses it**

**The hot path issue:** Line 70 in `results.py` passes `state_compat` as a parameter that isn't consumed. This is busywork-level — it's cargo cult code, not a performance problem.

**Verdict:** `cmd.py` and `flags.py` usage is legitimate (they actually call the functions). But the 40+ imports and facades are not actively used on the import execution path — it's just re-exported functionality. This could be collapsed but isn't urgent.

---

### 2. **What "state_compat" actually is**

`state_compat` is **not** a meaningless wrapper — it's a **documented compatibility facade** serving a real architectural purpose:

```python
"""Compatibility facade for legacy `desloppify.state` imports.

New code should prefer narrower modules such as `desloppify.state_io` and
`desloppify.state_score_snapshot`, but this module preserves the historical
surface for callers that still import the old umbrella API.
```

It re-exports 68 functions and types from across 9 internal modules:
- `engine._state.filtering`, `merge`, `noise`, `persistence`, `resolution`, `schema`, `schema_scores`
- `state_score_snapshot`

**Is it useful?** Yes — it prevents the API surface from fragmenting. But **is it needed in review/importing?** Partially. Some imports (like `utc_now()`) are legitimately used; others are passed around unnecessarily.

---

### 3. **Dual review judgment field names — THIS IS REAL**

Found in `/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/assessments.py` lines 27-54:

```python
# Accept dimension_character (new) or issue_character (legacy)
dimension_character = ""
dc = raw.get("dimension_character")
if isinstance(dc, str) and dc.strip():
    dimension_character = dc.strip()

issue_character = ""
ic = raw.get("issue_character")
if isinstance(ic, str) and ic.strip():
    issue_character = ic.strip()
```

And then when storing:

```python
# Store dimension_character, falling back to issue_character
effective_dim_char = dimension_character or issue_character
if effective_dim_char:
    result["dimension_character"] = effective_dim_char
if issue_character and not dimension_character:
    result["issue_character"] = issue_character
```

**What's happening:** The code accepts BOTH old (`issue_character`) and new (`dimension_character`) field names from incoming payloads, **normalizes to the new name for storage, but preserves the old name as a fallback.** This adds branching logic and makes the schema ambiguous.

**Is this necessary?** NO. The codebase has a stated backwards-compatibility policy (in CLAUDE.md):
> **No backward compat for import paths/APIs** — this is an internal tool, not a library. Remove re-export facades, wrapper shims, compat layers.

This legacy field acceptance violates that policy unless there's old live data that depends on it.

---

## Honest Assessment: Is This Busywork?

**Partially.** Here's the honest breakdown:

| Issue | Severity | Why |
|-------|----------|-----|
| `state_compat` unused parameter in results.py | **Trivial** | Just remove the `state_mod=state_compat` argument pass — < 10 char edit |
| Dual field names (`issue_character`/`dimension_character`) | **Real** | Adds branching, schema confusion, violates stated policy. But low-impact since the code normalizes them |
| Overall facade usage in review/importing | **Low** | Functions ARE called, so the facade isn't pointless; but consolidation would improve clarity |

**What would actually improve the code:**
1. Remove the unused `state_mod` parameter pass in `results.py` (1 line, trivial)
2. **Drop the `issue_character` fallback** in assessments.py (delete lines 33-36, 53-54, simplify logic) — this is the real fix
3. Consolidate imports in review/importing to use narrower modules instead of `state_compat` — this is medium effort but low value

**The honest answer:** This is **not** busywork, but it's **low-priority**. The actual payoff is:
- Clarity: better schema definition (no dual fields)
- Purity: consistency with stated backward-compat policy
- Code volume: ~20 lines removed

But it won't meaningfully impact performance, functionality, or reliability. This is polish, not problem-solving.

## Queue Item Evaluation Report

Based on code inspection, here's my assessment of these desloppify queue items:

### 1. "Language framework interfaces expose Any across core plugin boundaries"

**Status: NOT GENUINELY PROBLEMATIC — This is "types for the sake of types"**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/types.py` lines 50, 80-90, the `Any` uses are:

- **Line 50**: `run: Callable[[Path, LangRuntimeContract], tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, int]]]` — detector phases return raw issue dicts
- **Line 80**: `dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None` — the dependency graph is intentionally polymorphic across languages
- **Line 82**: `review_cache: dict[str, Any]` — language-specific review data cache
- **Line 84**: `subjective_assessments: dict[str, Any]` — subjective review results
- **Line 88-90**: `runtime_setting/runtime_option` methods returning `Any` — these are intentionally polymorphic config values

**Why it's not a real issue:** Each `Any` is *semantically* constrained by the context (detector raw entries are always dicts, dep_graph values are language-specific but always nested dicts, etc.). These aren't "unknown untyped boundaries" — they're *polymorphic* by design. Adding TypeVars or recursive types would make the Protocol unreadable and wouldn't catch any real errors (callers already know what they're putting in). **Don't fix this.**

---

### 2. "phase_security() hides a module-global dependency swap"

**Status: GENUINELY DANGEROUS — Real runtime mutation**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/shared_phases.py` lines 74-81:

```python
def phase_security(path: Path, lang: LangRuntimeContract) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
    """Compatibility wrapper with patchable security detector dependency."""
    original = shared_phases_review_mod.detect_security_issues
    shared_phases_review_mod.detect_security_issues = detect_security_issues  # MODULE-LEVEL MUTATION
    try:
        return shared_phases_review_mod.phase_security(path, lang)
    finally:
        shared_phases_review_mod.detect_security_issues = original
```

What it does: It **swaps a module-level function** on `shared_phases_review_mod` at runtime. This is a "seam injection" pattern for testing. The problem:

1. **Thread-unsafe** — if two threads call `phase_security()` simultaneously, the swap races and one thread gets the wrong detector
2. **Hidden mutation** — the behavior of `shared_phases_review_mod.phase_security()` depends on whether you've called this wrapper or not
3. **Tests that mock `detect_security_issues` at the module level won't work correctly** if they call this wrapper (the mock gets overwritten)

This is actual technical debt. The function at line 35 (`detect_security_issues = _detect_security_issues_default`) is already meant to be patchable, but line 77 mutates the *downstream* module instead of just using the module-level variable.

**Should be fixed.** Recommendation: Pass the detector function as a parameter to `shared_phases_review_mod.phase_security()` instead of mutating it.

---

### 3. "Legacy detector modules use older module style"

**Status: REAL BUT DOESN'T MATTER — Not a consistency problem that affects behavior**

Checking detector `__init__.py` files:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/__init__.py` — 0 lines (empty)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/go/detectors/__init__.py` — 0 lines (empty)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/__init__.py` — 1 line (empty after docstring removed or minimal)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/csharp/detectors/__init__.py` — 4 lines (minimal docstring)

The actual detector modules themselves (e.g., `deps.py`, `unused.py`, `complexity.py`) all use standard modern Python: `from __future__ import annotations`, type hints, proper docstrings.

There is **no "old module style" detected**. Detector modules are just functions and helper types, not classes. The inconsistency (if any) is just in how much the detector `__init__.py` files document themselves, which is cosmetic.

**Not a real issue to fix.**

---

### 4. "Plan corruption silently converted to empty plan"

**Status: NOT SILENT — Corruption is explicitly warned**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/persistence.py` lines 92-140:

```python
def load_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> PlanModel:
    """Load plan from disk, or return empty plan on missing/corruption."""
    plan_path = path or _default_plan_file()
    if not plan_path.exists():
        return empty_plan()

    try:
        data = json.loads(plan_path.read_text())
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
        # Try backup first
        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
        if backup.exists():
            try:
                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
                logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s), loaded from backup.", ex)
                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)  # EXPLICIT WARNING
            except ...:
                logger.warning("Plan file and backup both corrupted: ...")
                print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted (...). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)  # EXPLICIT WARNING
                return empty_plan()
        else:
            logger.warning("Plan file corrupted (%s). Starting fresh.", ex)
            print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)  # EXPLICIT WARNING
            return empty_plan()
```

**Corruption is NOT silent:**
- **Logger warnings** (lines 106, 110, 114) always fire
- **stderr messages** (lines 107, 111, 115) always print
- **Backup recovery** is attempted before giving up

The function also has validation (lines 118-140) that checks for invalid plan structure and warns before falling back to empty.

**This is actually correctly implemented.** The only possible improvement would be to make the backup restore more explicit in the return path, but it's not a silent corruption issue.

---

|------|-------|----------|---|
| #1: Any in boundaries | No | N/A | **Skip** — polymorphic by design |
| #2: phase_security module swap | **Yes** | Medium | **Yes** — thread-unsafe mutation pattern |
| #3: Detector module styles | No | N/A | **Skip** — cosmetic inconsistency only |
| #4: Plan corruption silent | No | N/A | **Skip** — corruption is explicitly warned |

Only item #2 is a real issue worth addressing.

---

## Analysis of Queue Items: Completion Strategy Filler Text, Observe Live State, and Conflicting Contracts

Based on my investigation of the codebase, here's my honest assessment:

### Issue 1: **Completion Strategy Filler Padding** - REAL ISSUE ✓

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline_completion.py`, lines 119-129

**The Problem:**
```python
def build_completion_strategy(stages_data: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]]) -> str:
    strategy_parts: list[str] = []
    for stage in STAGES:
        report = str(stages_data.get(stage, {}).get("report", ""))
        if report:
            strategy_parts.append(f"[{stage}] {report[:200]}")
    strategy = " ".join(strategy_parts)
    if len(strategy) < 200:
        strategy = strategy + " " + "Automated triage via codex subagent pipeline. " * 3
    return strategy
```

**Why this is a genuine issue:**
- When the combined stage reports are short (< 200 chars), the function pads with repeated filler text: `"Automated triage via codex subagent pipeline. "` repeated 3 times
- This is explicitly fabrication — adding meaningless repetition to artificially inflate length
- The test at line 549 actually asserts `len(strategy) >= 200`, which forces the padding behavior
- The strategy text is user-facing (it becomes the completion narrative) and reviewers/users will see this repetitive padding
- Better approach: synthesize a real summary from stage reports instead of padding with filler

**Verdict:** This is a **real quality problem**, not a nitpick. It's sloppy UX that reveals the automation boundary to users.

---

### Issue 2: **Observe Confirmation Uses Live State Instead of Frozen Snapshot** - REAL BUT SUBTLE ✓

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/confirmations/basic.py`, lines 186-245

**The Problem:**
```python
def confirm_observe(...) -> None:
    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
    si = resolved_services.collect_triage_input(plan, runtime.state)  # Line 200: LIVE STATE
    obs = stages["observe"]  # Line 201: FROZEN RECORD
    
    issue_count = obs.get("issue_count", len(si.open_issues))  # Mixes both!
    ...
```

**What the cluster action step says should happen:**
> "stop deriving observe confirmation summaries from `collect_triage_input(plan, runtime.state)`. Use the frozen observe record in `stages['observe']` plus the frozen `active_triage_issue_ids` metadata"

**Why this is a real issue:**
- The confirmation reads the **current live state** via `collect_triage_input()` (line 200), which invokes `build_triage_snapshot()` that reads the live open issues from state
- But the observe stage was already run and recorded at an earlier point in time, creating a **frozen snapshot** in `stages['observe']`
- If new issues appeared between observe recording and observe confirmation, the confirmation would silently use the new count
- The `issue_count = obs.get("issue_count", len(si.open_issues))` line (208) shows the mixing: prefers frozen count but falls back to live count
- This is a subtle correctness bug: confirmation validation should use the frozen observation, not live state drift

**Verdict:** This is a **real design bug** — confirmation should validate against the frozen record, not the live state which may have changed. It's subtle but could cause validation checks to silently pass/fail differently on a second run.

---

### Issue 3: **Observe Stage Exposes Two Conflicting Contracts** - DEBATABLE but REAL ✓

**The Two Contracts:**

1. **Stage command contract** (in `stages/commands.py`, `stages/observe.py`):
   - Signature: `cmd_stage_observe(args, *, services=TriageServices | None, ...)`
   - Called by the pipeline as: `cmd_stage_observe(record_args, services=services)` (orchestrator_codex_pipeline_execution.py:59)
   - Takes `args` with attributes like `report`, `attestation`

2. **Confirmation handler contract** (in `confirmations/basic.py`):
   - Signature: `confirm_observe(args, plan, stages, attestation, *, services=...)`
   - Takes explicit `plan` and `stages` dicts plus top-level `attestation`
   - Expected to derive things from `plan` and read frozen state in `stages`

**Why this is a real coherence issue:**
- The observe stage (`cmd_stage_observe`) loads the plan itself: `plan = resolved_services.load_plan()`
- The confirmation handler (`confirm_observe`) receives plan as a parameter: `plan: dict`
- These two functions have different calling conventions and expectations about where state comes from
- The stage command reads live state; the confirmation is supposed to read frozen state
- This contract split makes it easy to accidentally mix frozen and live state semantics (as Issue 2 shows)

**The actual problem isn't just contracts existing — it's that they're inconsistent:**
- Why does observe command load its own plan but confirmation receives plan as a parameter?
- Why doesn't observe pass plan/stages to the confirmation, instead having confirmation re-derive things?
- The split makes the data flow murky and error-prone

**Verdict:** This is a **real architectural smell**. The inconsistent contracts create opportunities for bugs (as evidenced by Issue 2) and make the code harder to understand. A cleaner design would either:
- Have stage commands always receive plan/stages explicitly (not load them)
- Or have both load from services consistently
- But mixing both patterns is a real coherence problem

---

## Overall Assessment

**Are these genuine improvements or just subjective nitpicks?**

- **Issue 1 (Filler padding):** **Genuine improvement** — this is objectively bad UX that reveals automation
- **Issue 2 (Live state vs frozen):** **Genuine bug** — confirmation validation could drift with live state changes  
- **Issue 3 (Conflicting contracts):** **Genuine design smell** — inconsistent calling conventions that enable bugs

All three are **real issues worth addressing**, not subjective nitpicks. The cluster action plan is well-justified.

However, the **priority level** matters:
- Issue 1 is a UX polish issue (moderate importance)
- Issue 2 is a correctness bug (higher importance)
- Issue 3 is an architectural coherence issue (enables future bugs, medium importance)

The suggested refactoring is solid but somewhat ambitious—start with Issue 2 (the actual correctness bug) and Issue 3 (contract coherence) before Issue 1 (filler text).

```
    57→    This Protocol lives here (rather than in runtime.py) to avoid an import
    58→    cycle: runtime.py imports concrete types from this module, and
    59→    DetectorPhase.run references LangRuntimeContract in its signature.
    60→    """
    61→
    62→    name: str
    63→    extensions: list[str]
    64→    entry_patterns: list[str]
    65→    barrel_names: set[str]
    66→    external_test_dirs: list[str]
    67→    test_file_extensions: list[str]
    68→    review_low_value_pattern: object | None
    69→    file_finder: FileFinder | None
    70→    extract_functions: FunctionExtractor | None
    71→    get_area: Callable[[str], str] | None
    72→    build_dep_graph: DepGraphBuilder
    73→    detect_lang_security_detailed: Callable[[list[str], FileZoneMap | None], LangSecurityResult]
    74→    detect_private_imports: Callable[[dict, FileZoneMap | None], tuple[list[dict], int]]
    75→    large_threshold: int
    76→    complexity_threshold: int
    77→    props_threshold: int
    78→
    79→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
    80→    dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None
    81→    complexity_map: dict[str, float]
    82→    review_cache: dict[str, Any]
    83→    review_max_age_days: int
    84→    subjective_assessments: dict[str, Any]
    85→    detector_coverage: dict[str, DetectorCoverageRecord]
    86→    coverage_warnings: list[DetectorCoverageRecord]
    87→
    88→    def runtime_setting(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any: ...
    89→
    90→    def runtime_option(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any: ...
    91→
    92→    def scan_coverage_prerequisites(self) -> list[DetectorCoverageStatus]: ...
    93→
    94→
    95→@dataclass
    96→class LangConfig:
    97→    """Language configuration — everything the pipeline needs to scan a codebase."""
    98→
    99→    name: str
   100→    extensions: list[str]
   101→    exclusions: list[str]
   102→    default_src: str  # relative to PROJECT_ROOT
   103→
   104→    # Dep graph builder (language-specific import parsing)
   105→    build_dep_graph: DepGraphBuilder
   106→
   107→    # Entry points (not orphaned even with 0 importers)
   108→    entry_patterns: list[str]
   109→    barrel_names: set[str]
   110→
   111→    # Detector phases (ordered)
   112→    phases: list[DetectorPhase] = field(default_factory=list)
   113→
   114→    # Fixer registry
   115→    fixers: dict[str, FixerConfig] = field(default_factory=dict)
   116→
   117→    # Area classification (project-specific grouping)
   118→    get_area: Callable[[str], str] | None = None
   119→
   120→    # Commands for `detect` subcommand (language-specific overrides)
   121→    # Keys serve as the valid detector name list.
   122→    detect_commands: dict[str, Callable[..., Any]] = field(default_factory=dict)
   123→
   124→    # Function extractor (for duplicate detection). Returns a list of FunctionInfo items.
   125→    extract_functions: FunctionExtractor | None = None
   126→
   127→    # Coupling boundaries (optional, project-specific)
   128→    boundaries: list[BoundaryRule] = field(default_factory=list)
   129→
   130→    # Unused detection tool command (for post-fix checklist)
   131→    typecheck_cmd: str = ""
   132→
   133→    # File finder: (path) -> list[str]
   134→    file_finder: FileFinder | None = None
   135→
   136→    # Structural analysis thresholds
   137→    large_threshold: int = 500
   138→    complexity_threshold: int = 15
   139→    props_threshold: int = 14
   140→    default_scan_profile: str = "full"
   141→
   142→    # Language-specific persisted settings and per-run runtime options.
   143→    setting_specs: dict[str, LangValueSpec] = field(default_factory=dict)
   144→    runtime_option_specs: dict[str, LangValueSpec] = field(default_factory=dict)
   145→
   146→    # Project-level files that indicate this language is present
   147→    detect_markers: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   148→
   149→    # External test discovery (outside scanned path)
   150→    external_test_dirs: list[str] = field(default_factory=lambda: ["tests", "test"])
   151→    test_file_extensions: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   152→
   153→    # Review-context language hooks
   154→    review_module_patterns_fn: Callable[[str], list[str]] | None = None
   155→    review_api_surface_fn: Callable[[dict[str, str]], dict] | None = None
   156→    review_guidance: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
   157→    review_low_value_pattern: object | None = None
   158→    holistic_review_dimensions: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   159→    migration_pattern_pairs: list[tuple[str, object, object]] = field(
   160→        default_factory=list
   161→    )
   162→    migration_mixed_extensions: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   163→
   164→    # Zone classification rules
   165→    zone_rules: list[ZoneRule] = field(default_factory=list)
   166→
   167→    # Integration depth: "full" | "standard" | "shallow" | "minimal"
   168→    integration_depth: str = "full"
   169→
   170→    _default_runtime_settings: dict[str, object] = field(
   171→        default_factory=dict, init=False, repr=False
   172→    )
   173→    _default_runtime_options: dict[str, object] = field(
   174→        default_factory=dict, init=False, repr=False
   175→    )
   176→
   177→    @staticmethod
   178→    def _clone_default(default: object) -> object:
   179→        return clone_default(default)
   180→
   181→    @classmethod
   182→    def _coerce_value(cls, raw: object, expected: type, default: object) -> object:
   183→        return coerce_value(raw, expected, default)
   184→
   185→    def normalize_settings(self, values: dict[str, object] | None) -> dict[str, object]:
   186→        return normalize_spec_values(values, self.setting_specs)
   187→
   188→    def normalize_runtime_options(
   189→        self,
   190→        values: dict[str, object] | None,
   191→        *,
   192→        strict: bool = False,
   193→    ) -> dict[str, object]:
   194→        return normalize_spec_values(
   195→            values,
   196→            self.runtime_option_specs,
   197→            strict=strict,
   198→            owner_name=self.name,
   199→        )
   200→
   201→    def set_runtime_context(
   202→        self,
   203→        *,
   204→        settings: dict[str, object] | None = None,
   205→        options: dict[str, object] | None = None,
   206→    ) -> None:
   207→        """Set default runtime settings/options for future LangRun creation."""
   208→        if settings is not None:
   209→            self._default_runtime_settings = self.normalize_settings(settings)
   210→        if options is not None:
   211→            self._default_runtime_options = self.normalize_runtime_options(options)
   212→
   213→    def runtime_setting(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   214→        """Read setting from config-level runtime defaults."""
   215→        return runtime_value(
   216→            self._default_runtime_settings,
   217→            self.setting_specs,
   218→            key,
   219→            default,
   220→        )
   221→
   222→    def runtime_option(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   223→        """Read option from config-level runtime defaults."""
   224→        return runtime_value(
   225→            self._default_runtime_options,
   226→            self.runtime_option_specs,
   227→            key,
   228→            default,
   229→        )
   230→
   231→    def detect_lang_security_detailed(
   232→        self,
   233→        files: list[str],
   234→        zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   235→    ) -> LangSecurityResult:
   236→        """Language-specific security checks with optional coverage metadata."""
   237→        return LangSecurityResult(entries=[], files_scanned=0)
   238→
   239→    def detect_private_imports(
   240→        self, graph: dict, zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
   241→    ) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   242→        """Language-specific private-import detection. Override in subclasses."""
   243→        return [], 0
   244→
   245→    def scan_coverage_prerequisites(self) -> list[DetectorCoverageStatus]:
   246→        """Optional preflight checks that can reduce scan confidence."""
   247→        return []
   248→
   249→
   250→__all__ = [
   251→    "BoundaryRule",
   252→    "CoverageStatus",
   253→    "DepGraphBuilder",
   254→    "DetectorCoverageRecord",
   255→    "DetectorCoverageStatus",
   256→    "DetectorPhase",
   257→    "FileFinder",
   258→    "FixerConfig",
   259→    "FixResult",
   260→    "FunctionExtractor",
   261→    "LangConfig",
   262→    "LangRuntimeContract",
   263→    "LangSecurityResult",
   264→    "LangValueSpec",
   265→    "ScanCoverageRecord",
   266→]
   267→
```

```
1→"""Review-oriented shared detector phases (dupes, security, coverage, etc.)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import log
     9→from desloppify.engine.detectors.dupes import detect_duplicates
    10→from desloppify.engine.detectors.jscpd_adapter import detect_with_jscpd
    11→from desloppify.engine.detectors.security.detector import detect_security_issues
    12→from desloppify.engine.detectors.test_coverage.detector import detect_test_coverage
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import make_issue
    14→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import EXCLUDED_ZONES, filter_entries
    15→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangRuntimeContract
    16→from desloppify.languages._framework.issue_factories import make_dupe_issues
    17→from desloppify.state_io import Issue
    18→
    19→from .shared_phases_helpers import (
    20→    _entries_to_issues,
    21→    _filter_boilerplate_entries_by_zone,
    22→    _find_external_test_files,
    23→    _log_phase_summary,
    24→    _record_detector_coverage,
    25→)
    26→
    27→
    28→def phase_dupes(path: Path, lang: LangRuntimeContract) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
    29→    """Shared phase runner: detect duplicate functions via lang.extract_functions."""
    30→    functions = lang.extract_functions(path)
    31→
    32→    if lang.zone_map is not None:
    33→        before = len(functions)
    34→        functions = [
    35→            function
    36→            for function in functions
    37→            if lang.zone_map.get(getattr(function, "file", "")) not in EXCLUDED_ZONES
    38→        ]
    39→        excluded = before - len(functions)
    40→        if excluded:
    41→            log(f"         zones: {excluded} functions excluded (non-production)")
    42→
    43→    entries, total_functions = detect_duplicates(functions)
    44→    issues = make_dupe_issues(entries, log)
    45→    return issues, {"dupes": total_functions}
    46→
    47→
    48→def phase_boilerplate_duplication(
    49→    path: Path,
    50→    lang: LangRuntimeContract,
    51→) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
    52→    """Shared phase runner: detect repeated boilerplate code via jscpd."""
    53→    entries = detect_with_jscpd(path)
    54→    if entries is None:
    55→        return [], {}
    56→    entries = _filter_boilerplate_entries_by_zone(entries, lang.zone_map)
    57→
    58→    issues: list[Issue] = []
    59→    for entry in entries:
    60→        locations = entry["locations"]
    61→        first = locations[0]
    62→        loc_preview = ", ".join(
    63→            f"{rel(item['file'])}:{item['line']}" for item in locations[:4]
    64→        )
    65→        if len(locations) > 4:
    66→            loc_preview += f", +{len(locations) - 4} more"
    67→        issues.append(
    68→            make_issue(
    69→                "boilerplate_duplication",
    70→                first["file"],
    71→                entry["id"],
    72→                tier=3,
    73→                confidence="medium",
    74→                summary=(
    75→                    f"Boilerplate block repeated across {entry['distinct_files']} files "
    76→                    f"(window {entry['window_size']} lines): {loc_preview}"
    77→                ),
    78→                detail={
    79→                    "distinct_files": entry["distinct_files"],
    80→                    "window_size": entry["window_size"],
    81→                    "locations": locations,
    82→                    "sample": entry["sample"],
    83→                },
    84→            )
    85→        )
    86→
    87→    if issues:
    88→        log(f"         boilerplate duplication: {len(issues)} clusters")
    89→    distinct_files = len({loc["file"] for entry in entries for loc in entry["locations"]})
    90→    return issues, {"boilerplate_duplication": distinct_files}
    91→
    92→
    93→def phase_security(path: Path, lang: LangRuntimeContract) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
    94→    """Shared phase: detect security issues (cross-language + lang-specific)."""
    95→    zone_map = lang.zone_map
    96→    files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    97→    entries, cross_lang_scanned = detect_security_issues(
    98→        files,
    99→        zone_map,
   100→        lang.name,
   101→        scan_root=path,
   102→    )
   103→    lang_scanned = 0
   104→
   105→    lang_result = lang.detect_lang_security_detailed(files, zone_map)
   106→    lang_entries = lang_result.entries
   107→    lang_scanned = max(0, int(lang_result.files_scanned))
   108→    _record_detector_coverage(lang, lang_result.coverage)
   109→    entries.extend(lang_entries)
   110→
   111→    entries = filter_entries(zone_map, entries, "security")
   112→    potential = max(cross_lang_scanned, lang_scanned)
   113→
   114→    results = _entries_to_issues(
   115→        "security",
   116→        entries,
   117→        include_zone=True,
   118→        zone_map=zone_map,
   119→    )
   120→    _log_phase_summary("security", results, potential, "files scanned")
   121→
   122→    if "security" not in lang.detector_coverage:
   123→        lang.detector_coverage["security"] = {
   124→            "detector": "security",
   125→            "status": "full",
   126→            "confidence": 1.0,
   127→            "summary": "Security coverage complete for enabled detectors.",
   128→            "impact": "",
   129→            "remediation": "",
   130→            "tool": "",
   131→            "reason": "",
   132→        }
   133→
   134→    return results, {"security": potential}
   135→
   136→
   137→def phase_test_coverage(
   138→    path: Path,
   139→    lang: LangRuntimeContract,
   140→) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
   141→    """Shared phase: detect test coverage gaps."""
   142→    zone_map = lang.zone_map
   143→    if zone_map is None:
   144→        return [], {}
   145→
   146→    graph = lang.dep_graph or lang.build_dep_graph(path)
   147→    extra = _find_external_test_files(path, lang)
   148→    entries, potential = detect_test_coverage(
   149→        graph,
   150→        zone_map,
   151→        lang.name,
   152→        extra_test_files=extra or None,
   153→        complexity_map=lang.complexity_map or None,
   154→    )
   155→    entries = filter_entries(zone_map, entries, "test_coverage")
   156→
   157→    results = _entries_to_issues("test_coverage", entries, default_name="")
   158→    _log_phase_summary("test coverage", results, potential, "production files")
   159→
   160→    return results, {"test_coverage": potential}
   161→
   162→
   163→def phase_private_imports(
   164→    path: Path,
   165→    lang: LangRuntimeContract,
   166→) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
   167→    """Shared phase: detect cross-module private imports."""
   168→    zone_map = lang.zone_map
   169→    graph = lang.dep_graph or lang.build_dep_graph(path)
   170→
   171→    entries, potential = lang.detect_private_imports(graph, zone_map)
   172→    entries = filter_entries(zone_map, entries, "private_imports")
   173→
   174→    results = _entries_to_issues("private_imports", entries)
   175→    _log_phase_summary("private imports", results, potential, "files scanned")
   176→
   177→    return results, {"private_imports": potential}
   178→
   179→
   180→def phase_subjective_review(
   181→    path: Path,
   182→    lang: LangRuntimeContract,
   183→) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
   184→    """Shared phase: detect subjective dimensions needing review.
   185→
   186→    Creates one issue per unassessed/stale subjective dimension instead of
   187→    per-file coverage markers.  The per-file review cache is still used by
   188→    ``review --prepare`` to know which files to queue, but does not generate
   189→    individual issues.
   190→    """
   191→    from desloppify.base.subjective_dimensions import (
   192→        default_dimension_keys_for_lang,
   193→        dimension_display_name,
   194→    )
   195→
   196→    assessments = lang.subjective_assessments if isinstance(lang.subjective_assessments, dict) else {}
   197→    default_dims = default_dimension_keys_for_lang(lang.name)
   198→    potential = len(default_dims)
   199→
   200→    results: list[Issue] = []
   201→    for dim_key in default_dims:
   202→        assessment = assessments.get(dim_key)
   203→        if isinstance(assessment, dict):
   204→            is_placeholder = (
   205→                assessment.get("placeholder") is True
   206→                or assessment.get("source") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   207→                or assessment.get("reset_by") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   208→            )
   209→            if not is_placeholder:
   210→                continue  # assessed and not stale — skip
   211→            reason = "stale"
   212→            summary = (
   213→                f"{dimension_display_name(dim_key, lang_name=lang.name)} — "
   214→                "assessment reset by scan, re-review recommended"
   215→            )
   216→        else:
   217→            reason = "unassessed"
   218→            summary = (
   219→                f"{dimension_display_name(dim_key, lang_name=lang.name)} — "
   220→                "no assessment on record, run `desloppify review --prepare`"
   221→            )
   222→
   223→        results.append(
   224→            make_issue(
   225→                "subjective_review",
   226→                ".",
   227→                dim_key,
   228→                tier=4,
   229→                confidence="low",
   230→                summary=summary,
   231→                detail={"reason": reason, "dimension": dim_key},
   232→            )
   233→        )
   234→
   235→    _log_phase_summary("subjective review", results, potential, "dimensions")
   236→
   237→    return results, {"subjective_review": potential}
   238→
   239→
   240→def phase_signature(path: Path, lang: LangRuntimeContract) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
   241→    """Shared phase runner: detect signature variance via lang.extract_functions."""
   242→    from desloppify.engine.detectors.signature import detect_signature_variance
   243→
   244→    functions = lang.extract_functions(path)
   245→
   246→    issues: list[Issue] = []
   247→    potentials: dict[str, int] = {}
   248→
   249→    if not functions:
   250→        return issues, potentials
   251→
   252→    entries, _total = detect_signature_variance(functions, min_occurrences=3)
   253→    for entry in entries:
   254→        issues.append(
   255→            make_issue(
   256→                "signature",
   257→                entry["files"][0],
   258→                f"signature_variance::{entry['name']}",
   259→                tier=3,
   260→                confidence="medium",
   261→                summary=(
   262→                    f"'{entry['name']}' has {entry['signature_count']} different signatures "
   263→                    f"across {entry['file_count']} files"
   264→                ),
   265→            )
   266→        )
   267→    if entries:
   268→        potentials["signature"] = len(entries)
   269→        log(f"         signature variance: {len(entries)}")
   270→
   271→    return issues, potentials
   272→
   273→
   274→__all__ = [
```

```
1→"""DetectorPhase builder helpers for shared framework phases."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from .shared_phases import (
     6→    phase_boilerplate_duplication,
     7→    phase_dupes,
     8→    phase_security,
     9→    phase_signature,
    10→    phase_subjective_review,
    11→    phase_test_coverage,
    12→)
    13→from .types import DetectorPhase
    14→
    15→
    16→def _make_detector_phase_factory(
    17→    label: str,
    18→    run_fn,
    19→    *,
    20→    slow: bool = False,
    21→    exclusive_detector: str | None = None,
    22→):
    23→    """Return a phase factory with explicit label/run/slow semantics.
    24→
    25→    *exclusive_detector*: if set, the detector module path that this phase owns.
    26→    Language-specific phase files must not import that module directly.
    27→    """
    28→    def factory() -> DetectorPhase:
    29→        return DetectorPhase(label, run_fn, slow=slow)
    30→    factory.exclusive_detector = exclusive_detector
    31→    return factory
    32→
    33→
    34→SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES = {
    35→    "test_coverage": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    36→        "Test coverage", phase_test_coverage,
    37→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.test_coverage",
    38→    ),
    39→    "security": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    40→        "Security", phase_security,
    41→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.security",
    42→    ),
    43→    "signature": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    44→        "Signature analysis", phase_signature,
    45→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.signature",
    46→    ),
    47→    "subjective_review": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    48→        "Subjective review", phase_subjective_review,
    49→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.review_coverage",
    50→    ),
    51→    "duplicates": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    52→        "Duplicates", phase_dupes, slow=True,
    53→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.dupes",
    54→    ),
    55→    "boilerplate_duplication": _make_detector_phase_factory(
    56→        "Boilerplate duplication", phase_boilerplate_duplication, slow=True,
    57→        exclusive_detector="desloppify.engine.detectors.jscpd_adapter",
    58→    ),
    59→}
    60→
    61→EXCLUSIVE_DETECTOR_MODULES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    62→    f.exclusive_detector
    63→    for f in SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES.values()
    64→    if f.exclusive_detector is not None
    65→)
    66→
    67→
    68→detector_phase_test_coverage = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["test_coverage"]
    69→detector_phase_security = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["security"]
    70→detector_phase_signature = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["signature"]
    71→detector_phase_subjective_review = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["subjective_review"]
    72→detector_phase_duplicates = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["duplicates"]
    73→detector_phase_boilerplate_duplication = SHARED_PHASE_FACTORIES["boilerplate_duplication"]
    74→
    75→
    76→def shared_subjective_duplicates_tail(
    77→    *,
    78→    pre_duplicates: list[DetectorPhase] | None = None,
    79→) -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    80→    """Shared review tail: subjective review, optional custom phases, then duplicates."""
    81→    phases = [detector_phase_subjective_review()]
    82→    if pre_duplicates:
    83→        phases.extend(pre_duplicates)
    84→    phases.append(detector_phase_boilerplate_duplication())
    85→    phases.append(detector_phase_duplicates())
    86→    return phases
    87→
    88→
    89→__all__ = [
    90→    "detector_phase_boilerplate_duplication",
    91→    "detector_phase_duplicates",
    92→    "detector_phase_security",
```

```
    17→def is_full_stage_run(stages_to_run: list[str]) -> bool:

    21→

    26→) -> bool:
    27→    """True when each requested stage is confirmed or already confirmed."""
    28→    for stage in stages_to_run:
    29→        status = str(stage_results.get(stage, {}).get("status", ""))
    30→        if status not in {"confirmed", "skipped"}:

    32→    return True

   100→        print(colorize(f"  Stage {stage}: confirmed ({elapsed}s).", "green"))
   101→        append_run_log(f"stage-confirmed stage={stage} elapsed={elapsed}s")

   112→    return (

   119→def build_completion_strategy(stages_data: Mapping[str, Mapping[str, Any]]) -> str:
   120→    """Derive a completion strategy from stage reports."""
   121→    strategy_parts: list[str] = []
   122→    for stage in STAGES:
   123→        report = str(stages_data.get(stage, {}).get("report", ""))
   124→        if report:
   125→            strategy_parts.append(f"[{stage}] {report[:200]}")
   126→    strategy = " ".join(strategy_parts)
   127→    if len(strategy) < 200:
   128→        strategy = strategy + " " + "Automated triage via codex subagent pipeline. " * 3
   129→    return strategy
   130→
   131→
   132→def complete_pipeline(
   133→    *,
   134→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   135→    services: TriageServices,
   136→    plan: Mapping[str, Any],
   137→    strategy: str,
   138→    triage_input: Any,
   139→) -> bool:
   140→    """Run the triage completion coordinator and report success."""
   141→    completed_before = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("last_completed_at")
   142→
   143→    print(colorize("\n  Completing triage...", "bold"))
   144→
   145→    attestation = build_auto_attestation("sense-check", plan, triage_input)
   146→    complete_args = argparse.Namespace(
   147→        complete=True,
   148→        strategy=strategy[:2000],
   149→        attestation=attestation,
   150→        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
   151→    )
   152→
   153→    from ..stages.completion import _cmd_triage_complete
   154→
   155→    _cmd_triage_complete(complete_args, services=services)
   156→
   157→    completed_after = (
   158→        services.load_plan().get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("last_completed_at")
   159→    )
   160→    return bool(completed_after and completed_after != completed_before)
   161→
```

```
1→"""Cross-language security detector entrypoint."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_scan_file
     9→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap
    10→
    11→from .filters import _is_test_file, _should_scan_file, _should_skip_line
    12→from .scanner import _scan_line_for_security_entries
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→
    17→def detect_security_issues(
    18→    files: list[str],
    19→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
    20→    lang_name: str,
    21→    *,
    22→    scan_root: Path | None = None,
    23→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    24→    """Detect cross-language security issues in source files."""
    25→    _ = lang_name
    26→    entries: list[dict] = []
    27→    scanned = 0
    28→
    29→    resolved_scan_root = scan_root.resolve() if isinstance(scan_root, Path) else Path.cwd()
    30→
    31→    for filepath in files:
    32→        if not _should_scan_file(filepath, zone_map):
    33→            continue
    34→
    35→        try:
    36→            resolved_path = resolve_scan_file(filepath, scan_root=resolved_scan_root)
    37→            content = resolved_path.read_text(errors="replace")
    38→        except OSError as exc:
    39→            logger.debug(
    40→                "Skipping unreadable file in security detector: %s (%s)", filepath, exc
    41→            )
    42→            continue
    43→
    44→        scanned += 1
    45→        lines = content.splitlines()
    46→        is_test = _is_test_file(filepath, zone_map)
    47→
    48→        for line_num, line in enumerate(lines, 1):
    49→            if _should_skip_line(line):
    50→                continue
    51→            entries.extend(
    52→                _scan_line_for_security_entries(
    53→                    filepath=filepath,
    54→                    line_num=line_num,
    55→                    line=line,
    56→                    is_test=is_test,
    57→                )
    58→            )
    59→
    60→    return entries, scanned
    61→
```

```
1→"""Basic triage stage confirmation handlers (observe/reflect + attestation parsing)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→from desloppify.base.output.user_message import print_user_message
     9→
    10→from .shared import (
    11→    StageConfirmationRequest,
    12→    ensure_stage_is_confirmable,
    13→    finalize_stage_confirmation,
    14→)
    15→from ..observe_batches import observe_dimension_breakdown
    16→from ..services import TriageServices, default_triage_services
    17→
    18→# Observe verdicts that trigger auto-skip on confirmation
    19→_AUTO_SKIP_VERDICTS = frozenset({"false positive", "exaggerated"})
    20→
    21→
    22→MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN = 80
    23→
    24→
    25→def _find_referenced_names(text: str, names: list[str] | None) -> list[str]:
    26→    if not names:
    27→        return []
    28→    return [
    29→        name for name in names
    30→        if name.lower().replace("_", " ") in text or name.lower() in text
    31→    ]
    32→
    33→
    34→def _validate_observe_attestation(text: str, dimensions: list[str] | None) -> str | None:
    35→    found = _find_referenced_names(text, dimensions)
    36→    if found or not dimensions:
    37→        return None
    38→    dim_list = ", ".join(dimensions[:6])
    39→    return f"Attestation must reference at least one dimension from the summary. Mention one of: {dim_list}"
    40→
    41→
    42→def _validate_reflect_attestation(
    43→    text: str,
    44→    *,
    45→    dimensions: list[str] | None,
    46→    cluster_names: list[str] | None,
    47→) -> str | None:
    48→    refs = _find_referenced_names(text, dimensions) + _find_referenced_names(text, cluster_names)
    49→    if refs or not (dimensions or cluster_names):
    50→        return None
    51→    return (
    52→        "Attestation must reference at least one dimension or cluster name.\n"
    53→        f"  Valid dimensions: {', '.join((dimensions or [])[:6])}\n"
    54→        f"  Valid clusters: {', '.join((cluster_names or [])[:6]) if cluster_names else '(none yet)'}"
    55→    )
    56→
    57→
    58→def _validate_cluster_attestation(
    59→    text: str,
    60→    *,
    61→    cluster_names: list[str] | None,
    62→    action: str,
    63→) -> str | None:
    64→    found = _find_referenced_names(text, cluster_names)
    65→    if found or not cluster_names:
    66→        return None
    67→    names = ", ".join(cluster_names[:6])
    68→    return f"Attestation must reference at least one cluster you {action}. Mention one of: {names}"
    69→
    70→
    71→def validate_attestation(
    72→    attestation: str,
    73→    stage: str,
    74→    *,
    75→    dimensions: list[str] | None = None,
    76→    cluster_names: list[str] | None = None,
    77→) -> str | None:
    78→    """Return error message if attestation doesn't reference required data."""
    79→    text = attestation.lower()
    80→    validators = {
    81→        "observe": lambda: _validate_observe_attestation(text, dimensions),
    82→        "reflect": lambda: _validate_reflect_attestation(
    83→            text,
    84→            dimensions=dimensions,
    85→            cluster_names=cluster_names,
    86→        ),
    87→        "organize": lambda: _validate_cluster_attestation(
    88→            text,
    89→            cluster_names=cluster_names,
    90→            action="organized",
    91→        ),
    92→        "enrich": lambda: _validate_cluster_attestation(
    93→            text,
    94→            cluster_names=cluster_names,
    95→            action="enriched",
    96→        ),
    97→        "sense-check": lambda: _validate_cluster_attestation(
    98→            text,
    99→            cluster_names=cluster_names,
   100→            action="sense-checked",
   101→        ),
   102→    }
   103→    validator = validators.get(stage)
   104→    return validator() if validator is not None else None
   105→
   106→
   107→def _apply_observe_auto_skips(
   108→    plan: dict,
   109→    meta: dict,
   110→    services: TriageServices,
   111→) -> int:
   112→    """Create skip entries for false-positive/exaggerated dispositions.
   113→
   114→    Returns the number of issues auto-skipped. Also updates the disposition
   115→    map with decision/target/decision_source and removes auto-skipped IDs
   116→    from queue_order.
   117→    """
   118→    from desloppify.state_io import utc_now
   119→
   120→    dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
   121→    if not dispositions:
   122→        return 0
   123→
   124→    skipped = plan.setdefault("skipped", {})
   125→    queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
   126→    count = 0
   127→
   128→    for issue_id, disp in dispositions.items():
   129→        verdict = disp.get("verdict", "")
   130→        if verdict not in _AUTO_SKIP_VERDICTS:
   131→            continue
   132→        # Don't double-skip
   133→        if issue_id in skipped:
   134→            continue
   135→        skipped[issue_id] = {
   136→            "issue_id": issue_id,
   137→            "kind": "triage_observe_auto",
   138→            "reason": verdict,
   139→            "note": disp.get("verdict_reasoning", ""),
   140→            "created_at": utc_now(),
   141→            "skipped_at_scan": 0,
   142→        }
   143→        # Update disposition with auto-skip decision
   144→        disp["decision"] = "skip"
   145→        disp["target"] = verdict
   146→        disp["decision_source"] = "observe_auto"
   147→        count += 1
   148→
   149→    # Remove auto-skipped IDs from queue_order
   150→    if count:
   151→        auto_skipped_ids = {
   152→            issue_id for issue_id, disp in dispositions.items()
   153→            if disp.get("decision_source") == "observe_auto"
   154→        }
   155→        plan["queue_order"] = [
   156→            fid for fid in queue_order if fid not in auto_skipped_ids
   157→        ]
   158→        services.save_plan(plan)
   159→
   160→    return count
   161→
   162→
   163→def _undo_observe_auto_skips(plan: dict, meta: dict) -> int:
   164→    """Remove previously auto-skipped entries before a fresh observe run.
   165→
   166→    Returns the number of entries un-skipped.
   167→    """
   168→    dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
   169→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   170→    count = 0
   171→
   172→    # Find all entries with decision_source == "observe_auto"
   173→    auto_skipped_ids = {
   174→        issue_id for issue_id, disp in dispositions.items()
   175→        if disp.get("decision_source") == "observe_auto"
   176→    }
   177→    for issue_id in auto_skipped_ids:
   178→        entry = skipped.get(issue_id)
   179→        if entry and entry.get("kind") == "triage_observe_auto":
   180→            del skipped[issue_id]
   181→            count += 1
   182→
   183→    return count
   184→
   185→
   186→def confirm_observe(
   187→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   188→    plan: dict,
   189→    stages: dict,
   190→    attestation: str | None,
   191→    *,
   192→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   193→) -> None:
   194→    """Show observe summary and record confirmation if attestation is valid."""
   195→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   196→    if not ensure_stage_is_confirmable(stages, stage="observe"):
   197→        return
   198→
   199→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   200→    si = resolved_services.collect_triage_input(plan, runtime.state)
   201→    obs = stages["observe"]
   202→
   203→    print(colorize("  Stage: OBSERVE — Analyse issues & spot contradictions", "bold"))
   204→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 54, "dim"))
   205→
   206→    by_dim, dim_names = observe_dimension_breakdown(si)
   207→
   208→    issue_count = obs.get("issue_count", len(si.open_issues))
   209→    print(f"  Your analysis covered {issue_count} issues across {len(by_dim)} dimensions:")
   210→    for dim in dim_names:
   211→        print(f"    {dim}: {by_dim[dim]} issues")
   212→
   213→    cited = obs.get("cited_ids", [])
   214→    if cited:
   215→        print(f"  You cited {len(cited)} issue IDs in your report.")
   216→
   217→    min_citations = min(5, max(1, issue_count // 10)) if issue_count > 0 else 0
   218→    if len(cited) < min_citations:
   219→        print(colorize(f"\n  Cannot confirm: only {len(cited)} issue ID(s) cited in report (need {min_citations}+).", "red"))
   220→        print(colorize("  Your observe report should reference specific issues by their hash IDs to prove", "dim"))
   221→        print(colorize("  you actually read them. Cite at least 10% of issues or 5, whichever is smaller.", "dim"))
   222→        print(colorize("  Re-record observe with more issue citations, then re-confirm.", "dim"))
   223→        return
   224→
   225→    if not finalize_stage_confirmation(
   226→        plan=plan,
   227→        stages=stages,
   228→        request=StageConfirmationRequest(
   229→            stage="observe",
   230→            attestation=attestation,
   231→            min_attestation_len=MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN,
   232→            command_hint='desloppify plan triage --confirm observe --attestation "I have thoroughly reviewed..."',
   233→            validation_stage="observe",
   234→            validate_attestation_fn=validate_attestation,
   235→            validation_kwargs={"dimensions": dim_names},
   236→            log_action="triage_confirm_observe",
   237→            not_satisfied_hint="If not, continue reviewing issues before reflecting.",
   238→        ),
   239→        services=resolved_services,
   240→    ):
   241→        return
   242→
   243→    # Auto-skip false-positive/exaggerated issues on observe confirmation
   244→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   245→    auto_skipped = _apply_observe_auto_skips(plan, meta, resolved_services)
   246→    if auto_skipped:
   247→        print(colorize(f"  Auto-skipped {auto_skipped} false-positive/exaggerated issue(s).", "green"))
   248→
   249→    print_user_message(
   250→        "Hey — observe is confirmed. Run `desloppify plan triage"
   251→        " --stage reflect --report \"...\"` next. No need to reply,"
   252→        " just keep going."
   253→    )
   254→
   255→
   256→def confirm_reflect(
   257→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   258→    plan: dict,
   259→    stages: dict,
   260→    attestation: str | None,
   261→    *,
   262→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   263→) -> None:
   264→    """Show reflect summary and record confirmation if attestation is valid."""
   265→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   266→    if not ensure_stage_is_confirmable(stages, stage="reflect"):
   267→        return
   268→
   269→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   270→    si = resolved_services.collect_triage_input(plan, runtime.state)
   271→    ref = stages["reflect"]
   272→
   273→    print(colorize("  Stage: REFLECT — Form strategy & present to user", "bold"))
   274→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 50, "dim"))
   275→
   276→    recurring = resolved_services.detect_recurring_patterns(si.open_issues, si.resolved_issues)
   277→    if recurring:
   278→        print(f"  Your strategy identified {len(recurring)} recurring dimension(s):")
   279→        for dim, info in sorted(recurring.items()):
   280→            resolved_count = len(info["resolved"])
   281→            open_count = len(info["open"])
   282→            label = "potential loop" if open_count >= resolved_count else "root cause unaddressed"
   283→            print(f"    {dim}: {resolved_count} resolved, {open_count} still open — {label}")
   284→    else:
   285→        print("  No recurring patterns detected.")
   286→
   287→    report = ref.get("report", "")
   288→    if report:
   289→        print()
   290→        print(colorize("  ┌─ Your strategy briefing ───────────────────────┐", "cyan"))
   291→        for line in report.strip().splitlines()[:8]:
   292→            print(colorize(f"  │ {line}", "cyan"))
   293→        if len(report.strip().splitlines()) > 8:
   294→            print(colorize("  │ ...", "cyan"))
   295→        print(colorize("  └" + "─" * 51 + "┘", "cyan"))
   296→
   297→    _by_dim, observe_dims = observe_dimension_breakdown(si)
   298→    reflect_dims = sorted(set((list(recurring.keys()) if recurring else []) + observe_dims))
   299→    reflect_clusters = [name for name in plan.get("clusters", {}) if not plan["clusters"][name].get("auto")]
   300→
   301→    if not finalize_stage_confirmation(
   302→        plan=plan,
   303→        stages=stages,
   304→        request=StageConfirmationRequest(
   305→            stage="reflect",
   306→            attestation=attestation,
   307→            min_attestation_len=MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN,
   308→            command_hint='desloppify plan triage --confirm reflect --attestation "My strategy accounts for..."',
   309→            validation_stage="reflect",
   310→            validate_attestation_fn=validate_attestation,
   311→            validation_kwargs={
   312→                "dimensions": reflect_dims,
   313→                "cluster_names": reflect_clusters,
   314→            },
   315→            log_action="triage_confirm_reflect",
   316→            not_satisfied_hint="If not, refine your strategy before organizing.",
   317→        ),
   318→        services=resolved_services,
   319→    ):
   320→        return
   321→    print_user_message(
   322→        "Hey — reflect is confirmed. Now create clusters, enrich"
   323→        " them with action steps, then run `desloppify plan triage"
   324→        " --stage organize --report \"...\"`. No need to reply,"
   325→        " just keep going."
   326→    )
   327→
   328→
   329→__all__ = ["MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN", "confirm_observe", "confirm_reflect", "validate_attestation"]
   330→
```

```
   139→def _validate_recurring_dimension_mentions(
```

```
    11→CoverageStatus = Literal["full", "reduced"]

    14→class DetectorCoverageRecord(TypedDict, total=False):

    16→
    17→    detector: str
    18→    status: CoverageStatus
    19→    confidence: float
    20→    summary: str
    21→    impact: str
    22→    remediation: str

    29→
    30→    status: CoverageStatus
    31→    confidence: float
    32→    detectors: dict[str, DetectorCoverageRecord]
    33→    warnings: list[DetectorCoverageRecord]

    38→class DetectorCoverageStatus:
```

```
1→"""Prompt/data contracts for whole-plan triage orchestration."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

    10→    PlanModel,

    14→from desloppify.engine._plan.triage.snapshot import build_triage_snapshot
```
